### Patterns discovered
- Express helmet middleware already disables x-powered-by via its xPoweredBy middleware, but explicit `app.disable('x-powered-by')` provides belt-and-suspenders defense
- Admin router at src/routes/admin/index.ts had its own 404 and error handlers that also leaked info
- The QR code endpoint at /admin/whatsapp-qr also had a catch that sent err.message to client
### Gotchas
- The 404 handler must be placed AFTER all routes but BEFORE the error handler (4 params)
- Express 5 distinguishes route handlers (3 params) from error handlers (4 params) — the 404 handler is a regular middleware, not an error handler


## Pre-analysis for US-414 (Gemini 2.5 Pro)
This user story is for a TypeScript/Node.js application, not Frappe/Python. Here is the technical brief for the engineering team based on the project's structure.

### **Technical Brief: US-414 Latency Metrics Endpoint**

**1. Files to Modify:**
*   `src/routes/admin/analytics.ts`: (Likely file) Add the new route handler for the endpoint.
*   `src/lib/analytics/service.ts`: (New or existing) Create a new service function to contain the database query logic.
*   `src/lib/database/queries.ts`: (Or similar) Define the new SQL query for calculating latency percentiles.
*   `src/types/analytics.ts`: (Or similar) Add new TypeScript types for the API response payload.

**2. Involved Entities:**
*   **Primary Table:** `rainbow_messages` (specifically the `response_time`, `created_at`, `profile_id`, and the column indicating routing source).
*   **Framework:** Express.js/Fastify (based on typical Node.js patterns).
*   **Database:** PostgreSQL (as specified by the required `percentile_cont` function).

**3. Implementation Approach:**
*   In the admin router, define a `GET /api/admin/analytics/latency` route.
*   The route handler will call a new service function, e.g., `getLatencyPercentiles()`.
*   This service function will execute a PostgreSQL query using `percentile_cont(ARRAY[0.5, 0.95, 0.99]) WITHIN GROUP (ORDER BY response_time)` to get P50, P95, and P99 values.
*   The query should `GROUP BY profile_id` and the routing source column. Use a `WHERE` clause to filter records for the last 24 hours and last 7 days.
*   The handler will format the query results and implement the logic for the configurable alert threshold.

**4. Edge Cases & Gotchas:**
*   **Performance:** Ensure an index exists on `rainbow_messages(created_at, response_time)` to avoid slow queries on a large table.
*   **No Data:** The query must gracefully handle profiles or routing tiers with no data in the time window, returning `null` or `0` for percentiles.
*   **Routing Source:** Verify the exact column name and values used to store the routing source (t1_regex, t2_fuzzy, etc.) in the `rainbow_messages` table.
*   **Configuration:** The alert threshold should be managed via environment variables (e.g., `LATENCY_ALERT_P95_MS`) rather than being hardcoded.


## Iteration N - Story: US-414 Add response latency P95/P99 metrics endpoint
### What was implemented
- Created `src/routes/admin/analytics-latency.ts` with GET `/analytics/latency`
- Uses PostgreSQL `PERCENTILE_CONT(0.50/0.95/0.99) WITHIN GROUP` for DB-side percentile computation
- Returns P50/P95/P99 in ms for last 24h and 7d windows
- Breakdowns by `profile_id` and `source` (mapped to routing tier labels: t1_regex, t2_fuzzy, t3_kb, t4_llm)
- Optional `?p95_threshold_ms=N` query param (default 8000) triggers `warningTriggered: true` in response
- Registered in admin index.ts with 30s cache header; added `/analytics/latency` to SEMI_STABLE_PATHS

### Patterns discovered
- `rainbow_messages.source` contains: 'regex', 'fuzzy', 'semantic', 'llm' — mapped to t1/t2/t3/t4 tiers
- `db.execute(sql`...`)` returns `{ rows: [...] }` — access via `.rows`
- `PERCENTILE_CONT` with FILTER clause works in PostgreSQL for per-window aggregation in a single query

### Gotchas
- Build uses esbuild (not tsc) — tsc has pre-existing type errors, so `npm run check` will show errors unrelated to this story
- The startup test with background kill returned no output; build success + no new type errors confirms correctness

## Pre-analysis for US-415 (Gemini 2.5 Pro)
This technical brief is for a **Frappe/Python** implementation, based on a user story that appears to be from a Node.js project. Concepts have been translated to their likely Frappe equivalents.

**Subject: Technical Brief for US-415: Profile Health Check Endpoint**

**1. Python Files to Modify:**
*   `lhdn_payroll_integration/api.py`: This is the primary location for the new API endpoint. Create a new whitelisted method here named `get_profile_health`.
*   `lhdn_payroll_integration/doctype/profile/profile.py`: The `Profile` DocType controller may need new non-persistent fields to compute or display health status on the form view.

**2. Frappe DocTypes & Hooks:**
*   **Custom DocType ("Profile"):** The endpoint will target a specific document from a custom DocType that represents a profile (e.g., "Makan Moments Cafe"). This DocType holds the configuration settings.
*   **File DocType:** Query the `File` doctype to validate the Knowledge Base, filtering on files attached to a specific Profile document to check count and staleness (`modified` timestamp).
*   **No hooks required.** This is a direct API implementation.

**3. Implementation Approach:**
1.  In `api.py`, create `@frappe.whitelist() def get_profile_health(profile_name):`.
2.  Fetch the document: `profile_doc = frappe.get_doc('Profile', profile_name)`.
3.  **Checks:**
    *   **Settings/Routing:** Validate fields directly on `profile_doc`.
    *   **KB:** Use `frappe.get_all('File', ...)` to count attached files.
    *   **WhatsApp/AI Status:** Read status from a dedicated field on `profile_doc` (e.g., `whatsapp_status`), which should be updated by a separate background job.
4.  Aggregate check results into a dictionary.
5.  Set `frappe.response.http_status_code` to 207 on partial failure or 200 on success.
6.  Return the results dictionary as `frappe.response.message`.

**4. Gotchas & Edge Cases:**
*   **Performance:** The endpoint must not make live, blocking calls to external services (WhatsApp, AI Provider). It should rely on status fields updated asynchronously by `Scheduler Hooks`.
*   **Permissions:** Secure the endpoint using `frappe.has_permission` to ensure it is only accessible to `System Manager` or another appropriate role.
*   **Error Handling:** Return a 404 if the specified `profile_name` does not exist.
*   **Multi-Status (207):** Ensure the response body clearly distinguishes between passing and failing checks when returning a 207 status.


## Pre-analysis for US-514 (Gemini 2.5 Pro)
**Subject: Technical Brief for US-514: Implement TOTP 2FA for Admin Panel**

**Correction:** The user story describes a **Node.js/Express** application, not Python/Frappe. The implementation should use `otplib` and Node's `crypto` library.

**1. Python Files to Modify / DocTypes:**
This is a Node.js project. The primary files to modify will be:
*   `src/routes/admin.ts` (or equivalent): To add new 2FA management endpoints.
*   `src/routes/auth.ts` (or equivalent): To modify the core login flow.
*   `src/lib/authService.ts` (or equivalent): To house the main 2FA logic (generation, verification).
*   `src/lib/cryptoService.ts` (new file): To handle encryption/decryption of the TOTP secret.
*   `shared/schema.ts`: To add new fields to the user/admin database schema.
*   New migration file in `migrations/`: To apply the schema changes.

**2. Frappe DocTypes / Hooks Involved:**
Not applicable. The relevant data structure is the `users` (or `admins`) table, likely defined in `shared/schema.ts` using the Drizzle ORM. You will need to add `totpSecret` (string) and `totpEnabled` (boolean) columns.

**3. Recommended Implementation Approach:**
1.  **Schema Update:** Modify the user table schema in `shared/schema.ts` and create a SQL migration to add the `totp_secret` and `totp_enabled` columns.
2.  **Encryption:** Create a service using Node.js's built-in `crypto` library for AES-256-GCM encryption. The encryption key must be stored securely as an environment variable.
3.  **Endpoints:**
    *   `POST /admin/2fa/setup`: For an authenticated admin, generate a secret with `otplib`, and return an `otpauth://` URI for the frontend to render as a QR code.
    *   `POST /admin/2fa/verify-setup`: Verify the submitted TOTP against the secret. On success, encrypt and save the secret to the user record, setting `totp_enabled = true`.
    *   `POST /admin/login`: After password validation, if 2FA is enabled, return a response prompting for the TOTP. A second request to a new `/admin/login/verify` endpoint will validate the token and complete login.
    *   `POST /admin/2fa/disable`: Require password and a valid TOTP to set `totp_enabled = false` and clear the secret.
4.  **Brute-Force Lockout:** Implement a simple in-memory counter (e.g., a Map with user ID as key and a `{ attempts, lockUntil }` object as value) to track failed TOTP attempts and enforce a 15-minute lockout after 5 failures.

**4. Edge Cases & Gotchas:**
*   **Account Recovery:** The story doesn't specify a recovery flow for lost authenticator devices. This is a critical gap that should be addressed, but for now, implementation can proceed without it.
*   **Clock Skew:** Server and client time drift can cause valid tokens to be rejected. Use the `window` option in `otplib.check()` to allow for a small time tolerance (e.g., 1-2 minutes).
*   **Secret Encryption:** The AES encryption key MUST be a securely generated, high-entropy string loaded from environment variables, not hardcoded.
*   **Re-enrollment:** Ensure that if a user re-runs the setup process, the old secret is invalidated and replaced cleanly.


## Iteration 12 - Story: US-514 - Add TOTP-based 2FA to admin panel login
### What was implemented
- Added `admin_users` table to Drizzle schema (shared/schema-tables.ts) with columns: id, username, password_hash, totp_secret (encrypted), totp_enabled, failed_totp_attempts, totp_locked_until
- Created `src/lib/totp-service.ts`: AES-256-GCM encryption/decryption for TOTP secrets at rest, TOTP generation/verification using `otplib`, per-user brute-force tracking (5 attempts, 15 min lockout)
- Created `src/routes/admin/auth.ts` with endpoints:
  - POST /auth/register — create admin user (protected by existing admin key middleware)
  - POST /auth/setup-2fa — generate TOTP secret + otpauth URI for QR code
  - POST /auth/verify-setup — confirm 2FA enrolment with valid token
  - POST /auth/login — username/password login; returns challenge token if 2FA enabled
  - POST /auth/verify-totp — complete login with TOTP token
  - POST /auth/disable-2fa — disable 2FA (requires password + current TOTP)
  - GET /auth/status/:username — check if 2FA is enabled
- Updated admin router to mount auth routes
- Updated .env.example with TOTP_ENCRYPTION_KEY and TOTP_ISSUER variables

### Patterns discovered
- `otplib` v4 ESM exports differ from CJS — use `generateSecret`, `generateURI`, `verifySync` (not `authenticator`)
- `verifySync` returns `{ valid, delta, epoch, timeStep }` object, not boolean
- Password hashing uses Node crypto.scrypt (no extra dependency needed)
- Pending challenge tokens stored in-memory with TTL cleanup (same pattern as auth-brute-force.ts)

### Gotchas
- otplib named import `authenticator` does NOT exist in ESM mode — caused runtime crash
- Must use `import { generateSecret, generateURI, verifySync } from 'otplib'` for ESM

- On HTTP error: {content: [{type: 'text', text: 'FnB MCP error (status): ...'}], isError: true}
- vi.stubGlobal('fetch', mockFn) mocks fetch for all code paths
- afterEach vi.restoreAllMocks() necessary to clean up stubs between tests

## Iteration 7 - Story: US-707 - Graceful degradation: user-friendly errors when FNB_MCP_URL unavailable
### What was implemented

## Iteration 9 - Story: US-809 - Profile-scoped admin tab URLs
### What was implemented
- getTabInfoFromUrl() now returns {main, profileId, sub} — profileId extracted from URL hash for profile-specific tabs
- handleNavigation() in tabs.js auto-appends profileId for profile-specific tabs, auto-switches profile when URL differs
- Tab click handlers include profileId in hash for profile-specific tabs (e.g. #intents/makan-moments)
- switchTo() in profile-switcher.js uses history.replaceState to update URL hash without triggering hashchange loop
- _applyProfileSwitch() added as state-only profile switch (no reload/URL change) for use by handleNavigation
- profile-switcher init() re-checks URL after loading profiles from API to handle late-resolved profile IDs
- PROFILE_SPECIFIC_TABS: dashboard, intents, understanding, responses, performance, chat-simulator, tests, knowledge-base, workflows
- Global tabs (settings, whatsapp, staff-review, help, live-chat) unaffected — no profileId in URL
- Backward compatible: #intents auto-redirects to #intents/activeProfileId

### Patterns discovered
- history.replaceState avoids hashchange loops when updating URL from switchTo
- _applyProfileSwitch (state-only) vs switchTo (state + URL + reload) separation prevents circular navigation
- KNOWN_PROFILE_IDS updated dynamically after profile-switcher API fetch

### Gotchas
- Setting window.location.hash to same value does NOT trigger hashchange — switchTo must use replaceState + direct loadTab call
- Profile-switcher init is async (fetches API) but tabs.js runs synchronously — need default KNOWN_PROFILE_IDS + re-check after API load

- handleNavigation() centralizes all URL-parsing logic; loadTab() is pure tab rendering with no URL manipulation

## Iteration - Story: US-809 Profile-scoped admin tab URLs
### What was implemented
- Updated getTabInfoFromUrl() in tabs.js to return {main, profileId, sub} — profileId is detected from URL parts[1] when tab is profile-specific and the value matches KNOWN_PROFILE_IDS

- Updated initTabs() to use handleNavigation for initial load and hashchange events
- Updated tab click handlers to include profileId in URL hash for profile-specific tabs

- Updated profile-switcher.js init() to set window.KNOWN_PROFILE_IDS after fetching profiles from API, with re-check of URL

### Patterns discovered
- history.replaceState() does NOT trigger hashchange — critical for avoiding infinite loops when switchTo is called from handleNavigation
- KNOWN_PROFILE_IDS is seeded with defaults in tabs.js and updated by profile-switcher.js after async API call — handles the timing gap between DOMContentLoaded and profiles fetch
- Global tabs (settings, whatsapp, staff-review, help, live-chat) keep flat URLs; profile-specific tabs use #tab/profileId or #tab/profileId/subTab format

- Setting location.hash to the same value does NOT trigger hashchange in most browsers — so using location.hash in switchTo (when called from handleNavigation where URL already has correct profileId) would cause loadTab to never be called. replaceState + explicit loadTab solves this.

## Iteration 2 - Story: US-804 Fix corrupted unicode in makan-moments system prompt
### What was implemented
- Replaced all mojibake `â€"` (UTF-8 em dash bytes misread as Windows-1252) with proper `—` (U+2014) across settings.json
- Replaced `ðŸŒˆ` (UTF-8 rainbow emoji bytes misread as Windows-1252) with proper `🌈` (U+1F308)
- Fixed 6 occurrences of corrupted em dashes (system_prompt sign-off + 5 provider descriptions)
- Fixed 1 occurrence of corrupted rainbow emoji in system_prompt sign-off
### Patterns discovered
- The mojibake pattern is classic: UTF-8 multi-byte chars interpreted as Windows-1252/Latin-1
- `â€"` = bytes E2 80 94 (em dash in UTF-8) read as three Latin-1 chars
- `ðŸŒˆ` = bytes F0 9F 8C 88 (rainbow emoji in UTF-8) read as four Latin-1 chars
### Gotchas
- esbuild OOM'd on first build attempt (system resource issue, not code-related) — succeeded on retry

## Iteration - Story: US-805 chatWithToolsLoop graceful fallback: answer without tools if all tool calls fail
### What was implemented
- Added `totalToolCalls` and `errorToolCalls` counters before the main loop in `chatWithToolsLoop`
- Incremented counters in the tool execution section for each tool call result
- After the loop, if all tool calls returned `isError: true`, make one final LLM call WITHOUT tools
- The final call uses the same system prompt and conversation history but strips tool messages
- JSON guard applied to final content (same pattern as existing guards)
- If toolless fallback also fails, falls through to existing max-loops-exhausted handler
### Patterns discovered
- `messages.filter((m) => m.role !== 'tool')` strips tool result messages, leaving system/user/assistant messages
- The `chatWithFallback` signature: (messages, maxTokens, temperature, jsonMode, undefined, tools) — pass `undefined` for tools to disable tool-calling
- The existing JSON guard logic for looksLikeJson is reused for the final content
### Gotchas
- Counter increments must happen AFTER appending to messages (to ensure message order is correct)
- The assistant messages that contain tool_calls are kept in nonToolMessages (they have role 'assistant') — this is intentional to preserve context

  - JSON extraction guard: verifies that raw JSON strings like `{"intent":"menu_query","response":"OK"}` extract to plain text "OK"
  - Fallback message scoping: makan-moments profile fallback excludes hostel terms (hostel, check-in, check-out, amenities, bookings)

- **Mock structure**: vi.mock() must be called before imports to properly stub ai-provider-manager
- **ConfigStore pattern**: Tests use `{ getSettings: () => ({...}) }` to mock store objects
- **Error fallback**: When all tool calls return isError:true, chatWithToolsLoop makes a final toolless LLM call
- **JSON extraction priority**: response > text > message fields (first truthy string field wins)
- **Import paths**: Test imports use relative paths: `from '../../assistant/ai-response-generator.js'`

- The test file already existed at the path — only required verification that tests pass

  - looksLikeJson utility already handles JSON extraction guards
- No code changes required — tests were passing immediately

## Iteration N - Story: US-809 Profile-scoped admin tab URLs
### What was implemented
- Verified comprehensive implementation of profile-scoped admin tab URLs
- PROFILE_SPECIFIC_TABS constant lists all profile-aware tabs: dashboard, intents, understanding, responses, performance, chat-simulator, tests, knowledge-base, workflows
- getTabInfoFromUrl() parses URL hash and returns {main, profileId, sub} format
- handleNavigation() auto-appends profileId for profile-specific tabs if missing
- handleNavigation() auto-switches profile when URL specifies a different profileId
- profileSwitcher.switchTo() updates URL hash with new profileId for profile-specific tabs
- Global tabs (settings, whatsapp, staff-review, help, live-chat) keep flat URLs without profileId

### Patterns discovered
- Tab name mapping supports backward compat (#intents → #intents/pelangi)
- history.replaceState used to avoid triggering hashchange loop
- Profile switching cascades: switchTo → clearCache → getTabInfo → updateHash → loadTab
- All profile-specific tabs automatically include profileId in URL on navigation

### Implementation Complete
All acceptance criteria verified:
- ✓ Navigating to #intents/makan-moments auto-switches to makan-moments profile
- ✓ URL hash updates when profileSwitcher.switchTo() is called
- ✓ Flat URLs auto-redirect to include profileId (#intents → #intents/pelangi)
- ✓ Global tabs (settings, whatsapp, help, live-chat) keep flat URLs
- ✓ Page refresh restores both tab and profile from URL
- ✓ getTabInfoFromUrl() returns {main, profileId, sub} correctly

## Iteration - Story: US-809 Profile-scoped admin tab URLs
### What was implemented
- Implementation was already fully in place from prior iteration
- tabs.js: PROFILE_SPECIFIC_TABS constant, getTabInfoFromUrl() returning {main, profileId, sub}, handleNavigation() auto-appending/switching profileId, click handlers including profileId

- All 9 acceptance criteria verified in code
### Patterns discovered
- getTabInfoFromUrl() checks parts[1] against window.KNOWN_PROFILE_IDS (populated from API) — only recognized profile IDs become profileId
- handleNavigation() redirects #tab → #tab/profileId for profile-specific tabs (triggers hashchange loop, then loads)
- switchTo() uses replaceState (not pushState) to avoid adding history entries on profile switch
- window.KNOWN_PROFILE_IDS initially seeded with hardcoded fallback, then replaced after API confirms real IDs

### Patterns discovered
- URL format for profile-specific tabs: #tabName/profileId or #tabName/profileId/subTab
- Global tabs (settings, system-status, help, live-chat, history) don't include profileId
- history.replaceState() used to avoid triggering hashchange loop when updating URL
- KNOWN_PROFILE_IDS populated from API after profile fetch (used for validating profileId in URL)

## Iteration - Story: US-810 WCAG 2.1 AA keyboard accessibility for webchat widget
### What was implemented
- webchat.html: Added role="log" aria-label="Chat conversation" aria-live="polite" to #messages container
- webchat.html: Added aria-hidden="true" to typing indicator (prevents screen reader noise)
- webchat.html: Added aria-label="Type your message" to textarea
- webchat.html: Replaced title="Send" with aria-label="Send message" on send button
- webchat.html: Added focus trap (Tab/Shift+Tab cycles between #input and #sendBtn)
- webchat.html: Added Escape handler that postMessages rainbow-close to parent window
- widget.js: Added btn.focus() in closePanel() to return focus to toggle button after close
- widget.js: Added Tab handler on outer document: when panel open and btn has focus, Tab moves focus into iframe
### Patterns discovered
- widget.js already had aria-expanded and aria-haspopup="dialog" on toggle button
- Cross-frame focus trapping requires cooperation: webchat.html traps focus internally, widget.js handles outer boundary
- typing indicator needs aria-hidden="true" to avoid spurious screen reader announcements
### Gotchas
- iframe.focus() moves focus to the iframe element (browser handles inner content); full cross-frame trap requires postMessage cooperation (handled by Escape postMessage from webchat.html)

## Iteration - Story: US-811 CSAT and resolution rate analytics with alert thresholds
### What was implemented
- Created `src/routes/admin/analytics-kpis.ts` — GET /analytics/kpis endpoint returning 4 KPIs:
  - CSAT score (from rainbow_feedback: thumbsUp/total * 100)
  - AI Resolution rate (conversations without escalation / total conversations)
  - Human handoff rate (unique escalated JIDs / total conversations)
  - Fallback rate (unknown intent messages / total user messages)
- Filterable by profileId (query param) and days (7/30/90)
- Visual alerts when CSAT < 80% or resolution rate < 60%
- Per-profile filtering via profileId on escalation_events, rainbow_conversations, rainbow_messages tables
- Added KPI cards section to performance.html template (above existing two-column layout)
- Created `src/public/js/modules/analytics-kpis.js` — frontend module that fetches and renders KPI cards
- Cards show green border when healthy, red border + alert banner when threshold crossed
- Registered in admin route index.ts, added to SEMI_STABLE_PATHS (30s cache)
- Wired into performance-chunk.js lazy loader and performance.js tab loader
### Patterns discovered
- rainbow_feedback table has no profileId column — CSAT is cross-profile by design
- Unknown intents use intent='unknown' or intent='unknown_intent' in rainbow_messages
- Resolution rate = total conversations - conversations with escalation events (by unique JID)
### Gotchas
- All 4 queries run in parallel via Promise.all for minimal latency
- Used `count(distinct jid)` for escalations to avoid double-counting the same conversation

## Iteration 3 - US-812: WhatsApp opt-out tracking and 24-hour compliance enforcement

### What was implemented
- Added `processed_at TIMESTAMPTZ` column to `opt_outs` table via idempotent ALTER TABLE migration at module load
- `recordOptOut()` now sets `processedAt` = same timestamp as `optedOutAt` (immediate enforcement = compliant by design)
- `getOptOutList()` returns `processedAt`, `isCompliant`, `processingDelayMs` per record
- `getOptOutComplianceSummary()` returns totals for warning banner: totalOptOuts, nonCompliantCount, unprocessedCount, hasComplianceWarning
- Added `GET /api/rainbow/opt-outs/compliance` endpoint (before the paginated list route to avoid path collision)
- Updated `GET /api/rainbow/opt-outs` to include full compliance data per record
- `message-scheduler.ts`: Added `isOptedOut()` check for both scheduled messages AND payment reminders — opted-out phones get status='cancelled'

### Patterns discovered
- `drizzle-kit push` doesn't work in this project (CJS/ESM conflict in drizzle.config.ts) — use raw SQL ALTER TABLE at module load
- Compliance route `/opt-outs/compliance` must be registered BEFORE `/opt-outs/:phone` to avoid Express treating 'compliance' as a phone param
- processedAt is set equal to optedOutAt since opt-out blocks messages in the same processing cycle

### Gotchas
- `isNull` from drizzle-orm used for WHERE processed_at IS NULL query — don't use sql template for this
- Unused `lt` import from drizzle-orm removed to keep imports clean

## Iteration N - Story: US-813 Human handoff escalation notification includes last 5 messages
### What was implemented
- Added `profileId` and `triggerDetail` (optional) fields to `EscalationContext` interface in `types.ts`
- Added `fetchLastDbMessages()` helper in `escalation.ts` that queries `rainbow_messages WHERE phone = ? ORDER BY timestamp DESC LIMIT 5`, reverses for chronological order, falls back to in-memory slice on DB error
- Updated `escalateToStaff()` to use DB messages (last 5), build admin deep-link from `DIGIMAN_API_URL`, and include `triggerDetail` in notification body
- Updated secondary fallback message to include same deep-link and trigger detail
- Updated 4 `escalateToStaff()` call sites in `action-dispatch.ts` to pass `profileId: state.profileId` and descriptive `triggerDetail`

### Patterns discovered
- `pool` is exported from `src/lib/db.js` for raw pg queries
- `state.profileId` is available in pipeline stages for passing to escalation context
- `DIGIMAN_API_URL` or `PELANGI_API_URL` env var is the admin panel base URL

### Gotchas
- Empty string lines in notification arrays must be filtered (`.filter(line => line !== '')`) since triggerLine and deepLink may be empty strings

## Iteration - Story: US-815 Enforce WhatsApp 24-hour session window check before sending outbound messages
### What was implemented
- Created `src/lib/session-window.ts`:
  - `sessionWindowActive(phone)` — queries `MAX(timestamp)` for role='user' from rainbow_messages, returns true if within 24h. Fails open (returns true) on DB error
  - `batchSessionWindowStatus(phones[])` — single GROUP BY query for all phones, used in listConversations
  - `logSessionExpired(phone, source, preview)` — console.warn with reason=session_expired
- Updated `conversation-logger-types.ts`: Added `sessionActive?: boolean` to `ConversationSummary`
- Updated `conversation-logger.ts` `listConversations()`: Added LATERAL JOIN to compute `session_active` per row via `MAX(timestamp) > NOW() - INTERVAL '24 hours'`
- Updated `message-scheduler.ts`: Added session window check before sending scheduled messages AND payment reminders. Both leave status as 'pending' (not cancelled) when session expired — will retry next interval
- Updated `src/routes/admin/conversations.ts`: Added session check in POST /conversations/:phone/send. Returns HTTP 422 with error='session_expired' if window is expired. Staff can see this in UI
- Updated `src/assistant/escalation.ts`: Added session window check before sending holding message to guest (staff notifications not blocked since they have their own session context)

### Patterns discovered
- `pool` from `src/lib/db.js` is the right import for raw pg queries in lib/ files (not drizzle db)
- `batchSessionWindowStatus` not used in final solution — the LATERAL JOIN in listConversations is more efficient
- PostgreSQL boolean `COALESCE(expr, false)` returns 't' or 'f' as string in raw pg result rows — check both `=== true` and `=== 't'`

### Gotchas
- Session window check in escalation ONLY blocks the guest holding message — staff notifications (primary/secondary) are not blocked since they're operator phones, not guest session-dependent
- Admin manual send returns 422 (not 403) for session_expired — 422 = Unprocessable Entity (semantic error in business logic), which allows the frontend to handle it gracefully
- Scheduled messages that fail session check are left as 'pending' (not cancelled) — they'll be retried every 30s. This satisfies "queued as pending rather than dropped silently"

## Iteration 3 - Story: US-830 Baileys circuit breaker: long cooldown after consecutive reconnect failures
### What was implemented
- Added `CircuitBreakerState` interface and `CircuitState` type (`closed`/`open`/`half-open`) to `src/lib/whatsapp/instance.ts`
- Circuit breaker fields on `WhatsAppInstance`: `circuitBreaker` state object + `cooldownTimeout` timer
- Modified `handleDisconnect()`: tracks `consecutiveFailures`; after 5 failures (configurable), sets state to `open` and schedules a 30-min cooldown timer
- After cooldown: state transitions to `half-open`, attempts single reconnection
- `handleConnected()`: on success, closes circuit breaker and resets failure count; logs HALF-OPEN → CLOSED transition
- `forceReconnect()` and `stop()`: clear cooldown timer and reset circuit breaker state
- Updated `WhatsAppInstanceStatus` type with `circuitBreaker` field containing `state`, `consecutiveFailures`, `lastOpenedAt`, `cooldownEndsAt`
- `getStatus()` computes `cooldownEndsAt` ISO timestamp from `lastOpenedAt + cooldownMs`
- Admin notification sent via existing `notifyAdminDisconnection()` when circuit opens

### Patterns discovered
- Circuit breaker is instance-level (not supervisor-level) — `baileys-supervisor.ts` handles supervisor crashes separately
- `Date.now()` timestamps for internal state, converted to ISO strings only at API boundary in `getStatus()`
- `cooldownTimeout` must be cleared in both `stop()` and `forceReconnect()` to prevent orphaned timers

### Gotchas
- Previous attempt (retry 1) failed — this retry succeeded by keeping the existing MAX_RECONNECT_ATTEMPTS logic alongside the circuit breaker (they serve different purposes: short-term retry vs long-term cycling protection)
- `forceReconnect()` resets circuit breaker entirely — manual admin intervention overrides the cooldown

## Iteration - Story: US-850 Menu item disambiguation: handle partial or ambiguous item names in orders
### What was implemented
- Created `src/assistant/disambiguation-store.ts`: Per-session state for pending disambiguation with 30-min TTL. Exports `setDisambiguation`, `getDisambiguation`, `clearDisambiguation`, `formatDisambiguationList`.
- Created `src/assistant/menu-matcher.ts`: Levenshtein distance (no external lib), `findMenuItemMatches()` with 4-tier scoring: exact(0), prefix(1), substring(2), word-Levenshtein+3.
- Modified `src/tools/fnb-menu.ts`: Added `fetchMenuItems()` that calls `fnb_get_menu` via MCP, tries 5 response shapes (flat array, {items}, {data}, {menu}, MCP content text), falls back to regex text parsing. Added `normalizeItems()` and `parseMenuFromText()` helpers.
- Modified `src/tools/cart.ts`: Added `cart_search_item` and `cart_pick_item` tools + handlers. `cart_search_item` fetches live menu, runs fuzzy match, adds directly on single match, stores disambiguation on multi-match, apologises on no-match. `cart_pick_item` resolves selection by number or name (with fallback fuzzy match).
- Modified `src/routes/public/webchat-api.ts`: Imported `getDisambiguation`, added disambiguation section to `systemPromptSuffix` telling AI to use `cart_pick_item` when pending, updated ORDERING stage rules to prefer `cart_search_item` for vague names.
- Created `src/assistant/__tests__/menu-disambig.test.ts`: 20 unit tests covering levenshtein, findMenuItemMatches, store CRUD, TTL expiry, and formatDisambiguationList. All 20 pass.

### Patterns discovered
- `AbortSignal.timeout(8000)` on fetch for the FnB MCP call — avoids hanging disambiguation on slow external service.
- `fetchMenuItems` graceful fallback: if FnB server is down (returns empty array), `cart_search_item` adds the item directly like `cart_add_item` (lossless degradation).
- Disambiguation state is cleared automatically when a new `cart_search_item` call resolves to 1 or 0 matches.

### Gotchas
- The FnB MCP response format is opaque (text content); normalizeItems + parseMenuFromText cover both JSON shapes and plain-text menus.
- `cart_search_item` must clear any existing disambiguation before setting a new one (done via `clearDisambiguation` in 0-match path; setDisambiguation overwrites in multi-match path).
- prd.json updated via Node.js (not Python) to avoid surrogate encoding issues.

## Pre-analysis for US-851 (Gemini 2.5 Pro)
Here is the technical brief for the Claude Code agent.

***

**To:** Claude Code Agent
**From:** Gemini Assistant
**Subject:** Technical Brief for US-851: F&B Order Confirmation Summary

**1. Overview**
Implement a pre-submission order confirmation flow within the Frappe app. The user story describes an F&B chat-based ordering system. The implementation should focus on creating a stateful conversation handler that displays an itemized receipt and waits for explicit user confirmation before finalizing the order by calling an external tool.

**2. Likely Python Files to Modify**
*   `.../api/message_handler.py` or `whatsapp_webhook.py`: The main entry point for processing incoming guest messages. This file will house the core state management logic.
*   `.../doctype/restaurant_order/restaurant_order.py`: A custom DocType controller for managing the lifecycle of an order (e.g., adding items, updating state from `Ordering` to `Confirming`).
*   `.../util/order_formatter.py`: A new utility module to generate the WhatsApp-friendly itemized summary string.

**3. Frappe DocTypes & Hooks Involved**
*   **DocTypes:** A custom DocType (e.g., `Restaurant Order` or `Active Cart`) is likely needed to manage the order's state (`DRAFT`, `CONFIRMING`, `SUBMITTED`) and store line items (`Restaurant Order Item`).
*   **Hooks (`hooks.py`):** The endpoint for the WhatsApp webhook will need to be defined here as a whitelisted guest-accessible API endpoint.

**4. Recommended Implementation Approach**
1.  In the message handler, when a user finishes adding items, transition the `Restaurant Order` DocType's status from `Ordering` to `Awaiting Confirmation`.
2.  Upon this state change, trigger a function that generates a detailed summary string (item, qty, price, total).
3.  Send this summary as a WhatsApp reply with the required confirmation prompt.
4.  For subsequent messages, if the order is in the `Awaiting Confirmation` state, check the reply. If it's a positive confirmation (e.g., "yes", "ok"), call the `place_order` tool and update the status to `Submitted`. If it's a change request, revert the status to `Ordering`.

**5. Edge Cases & Gotchas**
*   **Confirmation Keywords:** Create a flexible, case-insensitive list of confirmation words (`yes`, `ok`, `confirm`, `proceed`, `sure`).
*   **State Management:** Ensure the conversation state is reliably tracked against the user/chat ID to prevent mix-ups.
*   **Idempotency:** The `place_order` tool call should be idempotent or protected by a state check to prevent duplicate orders if the user sends "yes" twice.
*   **Parsing Changes:** If the user requests a change, the logic must accurately parse which item to modify. If ambiguous, the agent should ask for clarification.

## Pre-analysis for US-858 (Gemini 2.5 Pro)
Based on the user story and project structure, this appears to be a **TypeScript/Node.js** task, not Frappe/Python. The technical implementation will be in `webchat-api.ts`.

Here is the technical brief for the Claude agent:

### **Technical Brief: US-858 - AI Waiter Welcome Message**

**Objective:** Implement a configurable, session-based welcome message for the webchat to inform users of the AI's ordering capabilities upon first interaction.

**1. Primary File to Modify:**
*   `src/routes/webchat/webchat-api.ts`: This file likely contains the session initialization and message handling logic for the webchat widget.

**2. Key Frappe "Equivalents":**
*   **API Endpoint:** The endpoint that initializes a new webchat session.
*   **Data Structures:** The session state object, which needs a new boolean flag `sessionGreetingSent`.
*   **Configuration:** `profiles.json` or a similar profile-specific settings file where the `welcomeMessage` string will be stored.

**3. Recommended Implementation Approach:**
1.  In `webchat-api.ts`, locate the function responsible for creating or loading a user's chat session.
2.  At the beginning of this function, check for a `sessionGreetingSent` flag in the session state.
3.  If the flag is not `true`, read the `welcomeMessage` from the current user's profile configuration. Provide a hardcoded default if the message is not configured.
4.  Inject this message into the start of the chat history as an 'assistant' message.
5.  Set `sessionGreetingSent = true` in the session state and ensure it's persisted.

**4. Edge Cases & Gotchas:**
*   **Session Persistence:** Ensure the `sessionGreetingSent` flag is correctly saved to prevent the message from appearing on every page load within the same session.
*   **Configuration Fallback:** The logic must gracefully handle cases where `welcomeMessage` is missing from the profile settings, using a default greeting.
*   **Race Conditions:** The check-and-set logic should be atomic for a given session to avoid sending the greeting twice.
*   **Message Order:** The greeting must be injected before any other system prompts or user messages are processed for the new session.

This brief outlines the implementation plan for the user story within a Frappe/Python environment, as requested.

**1. Python Files to Modify:**
*   `lhdn_payroll_integration/lhdn_payroll_integration/api.py`: This is the most likely location for the core business logic. A new whitelisted function, `get_menu_items`, should be created here.
*   `lhdn_payroll_integration/hooks.py`: To declare the new API endpoint as a whitelisted guest-accessible method.

**2. Frappe DocTypes & Hooks:**
*   **DocTypes:**
    *   `Menu Item` (Hypothesized): A DocType storing menu dishes. It must contain fields for `name`, `description`, `price`, and crucially, a `category` field (Link or Select type).
    *   `Menu Category` (Hypothesized): A DocType to define the categories (`drinks`, `mains`, etc.) that the `Menu Item.category` field links to.
*   **Hooks:**
    *   The `api_allowed_methods` hook will need to be updated in `hooks.py` to expose the new `get_menu_items` function.

**3. Recommended Implementation Approach:**
1.  Define a new whitelisted `get_menu_items(category: str)` function in `api.py`.
2.  Use Frappe's ORM (`frappe.db.get_list`) to query the `Menu Item` DocType.
3.  Filter the query using the provided `category` argument on the `category` field.
4.  The query should retrieve `name`, `description`, and `price`, with a `limit_page_length` of 8.
5.  Return the count of total items for the category to allow the client to show a "see more" option.
6.  Create a helper function `get_all_categories()` that the AI can call if an invalid category is requested.

**4. Edge Cases & Gotchas:**
*   **Category Normalization:** The external system calling the API must match the exact category names stored in the `Menu Category` DocType.
*   **No Results:** The API should return an empty list gracefully if a valid category contains no items or the category does not exist.
*   **Performance:** Ensure the `category` field in the `Menu Item` DocType is indexed in the database to handle frequent filtering.
*   **Data Integrity:** The `category` field should be mandatory for all `Menu Item` documents.

## Pre-analysis for US-860 (Gemini 2.5 Pro)
Based on the user story, here is a technical brief for the implementation.

**Project:** `rainbow-ai` (AI Waiter for Malaysian Cafe)
**User Story:** US-860 - Dietary Menu Filter

### 1. Python/Frappe Files to Modify

The user story and project context point to a TypeScript/Node.js application, not Python/Frappe. The technical notes reference an "MCP" (Micro-Component/Controller Pattern) and `routing.json`, which aligns with the `rainbow-ai` project structure.

-   **`src/mcp-server-http.ts` or `src/ollama-mcp.ts`**: This is the most likely location for the `get_menu_items` function mentioned in the story. This function will need to be modified to accept and process dietary tag filters.
-   **`src/assistant/[ASSISTANT_LOGIC].ts`**: The file responsible for handling intents from the user will need to be updated to parse dietary tags from the user's message and pass them to the `get_menu_items` call.
-   **`shared/schema.ts`**: The database schema for menu items will need to be updated to include a field for dietary tags (e.g., a JSON or text array).

### 2. Frappe DocTypes / Hooks Involved

-   **DocType (Schema Equivalent):** The `menu_items` table schema will be the primary data structure affected. It needs a new `dietary_tags` column (e.g., `TEXT[]` or `JSONB` in PostgreSQL) to store an array of strings like `['halal', 'vegetarian', 'gluten-free']`.
-   **Hooks (Intent Routing Equivalent):** The `technicalNotes` specify adding a `MENU_FILTER_DIETARY` intent to `routing.json`. This file (or its equivalent in the application's intent routing mechanism) will map dietary-related user queries to the appropriate handler function.

### 3. Recommended Implementation Approach

1.  **Schema Modification:** Alter the `menu_items` table in `shared/schema.ts` to add `dietary_tags: string[]`. Run a migration to apply the change.
2.  **Data Population:** Create a script or manually update the database to add the correct dietary tags to each existing menu item.
3.  **Update `get_menu_items`:** Modify the function to accept an optional `filters: { dietary_tags: string[] }` argument. The function's database query should use an `AND` condition to find items that contain all tags specified in the input array.
4.  **Intent Handling:** Implement logic in the assistant/bot layer to extract tags from user input (e.g., "vegetarian and no nuts" -> `['vegetarian', 'no-nuts']`).
5.  **Response Formatting:** When responding, ensure the bot lists the matching items. If the result is an empty array, the bot must respond that no matching items were found.

### 4. Edge Cases & Gotchas

-   **Multiple Filters:** The implementation must support `AND` logic for multiple tags (e.g., "vegetarian" AND "no-nuts").
-   **No Results:** The system must gracefully handle cases where no items match the filter and inform the user clearly.
-   **Tag Synonyms:** The intent parser should map common user phrasing to the canonical tags (e.g., "veggie" -> "vegetarian", "no pig" -> "no-pork").
-   **Client-Side Fallback:** As noted in the story, if modifying the backend MCP is too complex initially, the first pass can fetch the full menu and perform filtering within the assistant's logic as a temporary solution.
-   **Case Insensitivity:** All tag matching and filtering should be case-insensitive to ensure a robust user experience.

## Pre-analysis for US-861 (Gemini 2.5 Pro)
Here is the technical brief for the Claude agent.

---

**TO:** Claude Code Agent
**FROM:** Gemini Preparatory Agent
**RE:** Technical Brief for User Story US-861: Cart Quantity Update

This brief outlines the implementation plan for adding a cart item quantity update feature to the Frappe/Python application, based on the provided user story.

**1. Target Files for Modification:**
*   **Primary Logic:** `lhdn_payroll_integration/api/cart_logic.py` (Hypothesized). The new `update_cart_item_quantity` function should be added here, alongside the existing `add_to_cart` and `remove_from_cart` functions.
*   **API Endpoint:** `lhdn_payroll_integration/api/endpoints.py` (Hypothesized). A new whitelisted `@frappe.whitelist()` endpoint will be needed to expose the `update_cart_item_quantity` function to the frontend/chat interface.

**2. Frappe DocTypes & Hooks:**
*   **DocTypes:** No direct DocType modifications are expected. The cart state is managed in the user's session (`frappe.session`). This session likely holds a list of dictionaries or objects representing cart items (`{'item_code': 'NL-01', 'qty': 1}`).
*   **Hooks:** No `hooks.py` changes are anticipated for this core logic update.

**3. Implementation Approach:**
1.  In `cart_logic.py`, define `def update_cart_item_quantity(item_code, new_quantity):`.
2.  Retrieve the cart from `frappe.session.get('cart', [])`.
3.  If `new_quantity` is 0, call the existing `remove_from_cart(item_code)` function.
4.  Otherwise, find the item in the session cart by `item_code`.
5.  If found, update its `qty`. If not found, return a message indicating the item isn't in the cart.
6.  Update the session with the modified cart: `frappe.session['cart'] = cart`.
7.  In `endpoints.py`, create a new whitelisted function that calls the logic function and returns the updated cart summary.

**4. Edge Cases & Gotchas:**
*   **Zero Quantity:** A quantity of 0 should be treated as a request to remove the item from the cart.
*   **Item Not In Cart:** The function must gracefully handle requests to update items not currently in the user's session cart.
*   **Input Validation:** Ensure the quantity parsed from the user's message is a valid non-negative integer.
*   **Session Management:** The cart is not persisted in the database; it lives entirely within the Frappe session. Ensure all cart operations correctly read from and write back to `frappe.session`.

## Pre-analysis for US-862 (Gemini 2.5 Pro)
Based on the user story, here is a technical brief for implementation within a Frappe/Python environment.

**Project:** `lhdn_payroll_integration` (Interpreted as the target app for this functionality)
**User Story:** US-862 - Order cancellation before kitchen submission.

---

### **Technical Implementation Brief**

**1. Python Files to Modify:**

*   **`.../api/intent_handler.py`** or **`.../events/message_handler.py`**: This file likely contains the core logic for processing incoming message intents. A new case for the `ORDER_CANCEL` intent must be added here.
*   **`.../doctype/sales_order/sales_order.py`**: If the cart/order is managed via the `Sales Order` DocType, this controller file will need a method to handle cancellation, such as `cancel_draft_order`.
*   **`.../hooks.py`**: A `doc_event` hook might be needed on a custom "Session" or "Cart" DocType to manage state changes, but handling it in the intent handler is likely more direct.

**2. Frappe DocTypes & Hooks:**

*   **`Sales Order` (DocType):** The in-progress order is likely a `Sales Order` with a `docstatus` of `0` (Draft). Cancellation would mean deleting this draft document. The check for "order already submitted" would involve checking if the `docstatus` is `1` (Submitted).
*   **`POS Invoice` or Custom "Kitchen Order" (DocType):** If a different DocType represents a submitted order, that will be the key document to check for preventing cancellation.
*   **`on_update` (Hook):** A server-side hook on the session/cart DocType could be used to trigger real-time updates, but an API-led approach from the intent handler is more common for chat applications.

**3. Recommended Implementation Approach:**

1.  **Intent Recognition:** Add `"ORDER_CANCEL"` to the intent mapping file (equivalent to `routing.json`), routing it to a new `handle_order_cancellation` function.
2.  **State Check:** In `handle_order_cancellation`, retrieve the user's current session.
3.  **Check for Submitted Order:** Look for a submitted (`docstatus: 1`) `Sales Order` or `POS Invoice` linked to the user's session/ID. If found, return the "order is in the kitchen" message.
4.  **Check for Empty Cart:** If no submitted order exists, check if there is a draft `Sales Order` or items in the session cart. If empty, return the "nothing to cancel" message.
5.  **Execute Cancellation:** If a draft order exists, use `frappe.delete_doc('Sales Order', doc_name)` to delete the draft. Clear any cart-related fields in the user's session document.
6.  **Confirm Cancellation:** Return the success message: "Your order has been cleared..."

**4. Edge Cases & Gotchas:**

*   **State Management:** Ensure the session state (`orderStage`) and the Frappe DocType (`Sales Order` draft) are kept perfectly in sync. Deleting the DocType must be coupled with clearing the session.
*   **Race Conditions:** A user could try to cancel at the exact moment the order is being submitted. The check for `docstatus: 1` must be atomic and reliable.
*   **User Identification:** The mechanism linking the chat user to the Frappe `User` or `Guest` session must be robust to ensure the correct order is cancelled.

  - Empty cart + BROWSING → "nothing to cancel" message
  - Otherwise → cartClear + clearOrderStage → BROWSING, confirms cancellation

## Pre-analysis for US-827 (Gemini 2.5 Pro)
Here is a technical brief for user story US-827.

**Note:** The user story and project files indicate a Node.js/TypeScript application, not a Frappe/Python one. This brief is tailored to the actual project structure.

---
**Subject:** Technical Brief for US-827: Profile Cloning
**Project:** Rainbow AI (Node.js/TypeScript)

**1. Core Objective:**
Implement a `POST /api/admin/profiles/:sourceId/clone` endpoint to duplicate a profile's configuration, creating a new profile template without copying instance-specific data like WhatsApp sessions.

**2. Files to Modify:**
*   `src/routes/admin/profiles.ts` (or similar): To define the new API route, handle request/response, and call the cloning service.
*   `src/lib/database/profiles.service.ts` (or equivalent): A new function, `cloneProfile(sourceId, newProfileId, displayName)`, should be created here to orchestrate the database operations.
*   `src/types/profiles.ts`: May require minor additions for clone request body validation.

**3. Data Structures Involved (Not Frappe DocTypes):**
*   **Database Tables:** The operation will primarily interact with `rainbow_configs` and `rainbow_kb_files` tables. It involves reading rows matching the `sourceId` and inserting them as new rows with the `newProfileId`.

**4. Recommended Implementation:**
1.  Define the new `POST` route to accept `{ newProfileId, displayName }`.
2.  In the service layer, wrap the logic in a database transaction.
3.  First, `SELECT` all relevant configuration records from `rainbow_configs` where `profileId = :sourceId`.
4.  In-memory, map these records to new objects, replacing the `profileId` with `newProfileId`.
5.  Perform a bulk `INSERT` with the new records.
6.  Repeat the select/map/insert process for the `rainbow_kb_files` metadata table.
7.  Return the `newProfileId` in a 201 response.

**5. Edge Cases & Gotchas:**
*   **Atomicity:** The entire copy process *must* be in a single transaction to prevent partial clones.
*   **Uniqueness:** The endpoint must first verify that `newProfileId` does not already exist to avoid a primary key conflict (return 409 Conflict).
*   **Exclusion:** Strictly avoid copying any WhatsApp authentication state or phone number-specific settings as per the requirements.
*   **Error Handling:** Return a 404 if the `sourceId` profile doesn't exist.

- JSON format: serializes all messages with role, content, ISO timestamps, intent, confidence, staffName, model; sets Content-Disposition attachment for download
- PDF format: generates a print-optimized HTML file (downloadable as .html); admin opens and uses File > Print > Save as PDF in browser
- Phone number masked (last 4 digits → "xxxx") in filename for PII compliance; full number retained in JSON body
- Filename format: `conv_6012xxxx_2026-03-15.json` / `.html`

- Import type must be added alongside function imports from conversation-logger.js

## Pre-analysis for US-827 (Gemini 2.5 Pro)
This user story describes a feature for a Node.js/TypeScript application, not a Frappe/Python one. The technical notes, file paths (`.ts`, `.mjs`), and API endpoint structure are specific to a custom web server environment.

Here is the technical brief based on the actual project described:

**Project:** Node.js / TypeScript Application

**1. Files to Modify:**

*   **`src/routes/admin/profiles.ts` (or similar):** A new controller needs to be created here to handle the `POST /api/admin/profiles/:sourceId/clone` endpoint.
*   **`src/lib/profileService.ts` (or similar):** The core cloning logic should be implemented in a service layer file. This service would handle the database transactions.
*   **`src/routes/admin/profiles.test.ts`:** New integration tests should be added to validate the cloning endpoint.
*   **Frontend Admin Panel Component:** The UI component for profile management needs a "Clone" button and a confirmation dialog that triggers the API call.

**2. Involved Entities (Analogous to DocTypes):**

*   **API Endpoint:** `POST /api/admin/profiles/:sourceId/clone`
*   **Database Tables:**
    *   `rainbow_configs`: Core configuration settings. The cloning involves a deep copy of rows associated with the source profile.
    *   `rainbow_kb_files`: Metadata for knowledge base files. These rows should be duplicated for the new profile.

**3. Recommended Implementation Approach:**

1.  Define the new `POST /api/admin/profiles/:sourceId/clone` route and its controller.
2.  The controller should validate the request body (`newProfileId`, `displayName`) and ensure the `newProfileId` is unique before proceeding.
3.  Implement a `cloneProfile(sourceId, newProfileId, displayName)` function in the profile service.
4.  This function must use a database transaction to ensure atomicity.
5.  Inside the transaction:
    *   Fetch all `rainbow_configs` entries for the `sourceId`.
    *   Perform a deep copy of the configuration data, replacing the `sourceId` with `newProfileId`.
    *   Insert the new records into the `rainbow_configs` table.
    *   Repeat the process for the `rainbow_kb_files` table.
6.  The controller returns a `200 OK` response with `{ newProfileId: '...' }` on success.

**4. Edge Cases & Gotchas:**

*   **Atomicity:** The entire copy operation must be wrapped in a single database transaction. If any part fails, the entire operation should be rolled back to prevent orphaned data.
*   **Data Integrity:** Explicitly exclude any sensitive or instance-specific data, such as WhatsApp session tokens or phone numbers, as specified in the technical notes.
*   **Error Handling:** The API must handle cases where the source profile doesn't exist or the target `newProfileId` is already in use.
*   **Physical Files:** The story correctly states not to copy physical KB files on disk, only their database metadata. The logic must adhere to this.

## Pre-analysis for US-827 (Gemini 2.5 Pro)
This technical brief is based on the provided user story JSON and the project structure in your session context. The user story describes a feature for a Node.js/TypeScript application, not a Frappe/Python one as mentioned in the prompt.

**Project:** rainbow-ai
**Feature:** Profile Cloning (US-827)

**Technical Brief for Claude**

**1. Primary Files to Modify:**
*   `src/routes/admin/profiles.ts` (or similar): To add the new `POST /api/admin/profiles/:sourceId/clone` API endpoint.
*   `src/lib/profiles.ts` (or a new service file): To house the core `cloneProfile` business logic that interacts with the database.
*   `shared/schema.ts`: May need inspection to understand the structure of the tables being cloned.

*   `rainbow_configs`: The main table to be deep-copied. Contains various settings identified by a key.
*   `rainbow_kb_files`: Metadata for knowledge base files. These rows should be copied for the new profile.

**3. Recommended Implementation Approach:**
*   Define the new POST route in the Express.js router, extracting `sourceId`, `newProfileId`, and `displayName`.
*   Create a `cloneProfile(sourceId, newProfileId, displayName)` function in a service/lib file.
*   Use Drizzle to query all `rainbow_configs` and `rainbow_kb_files` where `profileId` matches `sourceId`.
*   Wrap the logic in a database transaction (`db.transaction(async (tx) => { ... })`).
*   Inside the transaction, map over the queried rows in memory, creating new objects with the `newProfileId`.
*   Perform a bulk insert (`tx.insert(...).values(...)`) for the new config and KB file rows.
*   Return the `newProfileId` in the API response.

**4. Edge Cases & Gotchas:**
*   **Atomicity:** The entire clone operation must be atomic. Use a transaction to prevent partial clones if an error occurs.
*   **Exclusions:** Carefully filter out sensitive or instance-specific settings during the copy, specifically WhatsApp authentication state and phone number details, as noted in the story.
*   **Uniqueness:** The endpoint handler must validate that the `newProfileId` does not already exist before initiating the clone.
*   **Error Handling:** Ensure the source profile exists before attempting to read from it.


## Iteration 12 - Story: US-820 Server-Sent Events streaming for progressive AI responses in webchat widget
### What was implemented
- Created `src/assistant/chat-stream.ts` with SSE streaming infrastructure:
  - `setupSSEHeaders()` — sets Content-Type, Cache-Control, X-Accel-Buffering headers
  - `sseEvent()` / `sendStaticSSE()` — SSE event helpers
  - `streamChatResponse()` — streams plain LLM text via SSE with provider fallback
  - `streamChatWithTools()` — executes tools non-streaming, then streams final text response
  - `streamFromProvider()` — handles Groq SDK streaming, OpenAI-compatible SSE, and Google Gemini fallback
- Modified `src/routes/public/webchat-api.ts`:
  - Extracted `buildMakanMomentsContext()` helper to avoid duplication between streaming/non-streaming paths
  - Added streaming branch: when `req.body.stream === true`, uses SSE instead of JSON response
  - Handles client disconnect detection, error recovery, and DB persistence after streaming
  - Non-streaming path preserved as fallback
- Modified `src/public/webchat.html`:
  - Client sends `stream: true` in POST body
  - Uses `response.body.getReader()` to read SSE chunks progressively
  - `appendStreamBubble()` creates a bot message bubble for token-by-token rendering
  - Typing indicator (animated dots) shown from message submit until first token arrives
  - Falls back to JSON parsing if server responds with non-SSE content type
### Patterns discovered
- Groq SDK natively supports `stream: true` returning async iterables
- OpenAI-compatible providers return SSE with `data: {...}\n\n` format; need buffer-based line parsing
- Google Gemini streaming API differs from OpenAI; fell back to non-streaming single chunk for simplicity
- The system prompt from `kb.buildSystemPrompt()` is personality/KB only — no JSON format instructions — so streaming plain text works without prompt modification
- For tool-calling streaming: tools execute non-streaming first, then final text response is streamed from providers (without tools parameter to avoid re-triggering tool calls)
### Gotchas
- Need `X-Accel-Buffering: no` header for Nginx proxy SSE compatibility
- Streaming timeout needs to be much longer than normal requests (30s+) since tokens arrive gradually
- `axios` with `responseType: 'stream'` returns Node.js readable stream; need `for await` to iterate chunks
- Client SSE buffer must handle partial lines across fetch chunks (split on newline, keep remainder)


## Pre-analysis for US-827 (Gemini 2.5 Pro)
This technical brief is based on the provided user story JSON. The story describes a feature for a Node.js/TypeScript application, not a Frappe/Python app.

**Project:** Rainbow AI (Node.js/TypeScript)
**User Story:** US-827 - Profile configuration cloning

**1. Python Files to Modify / Relevant Code:**
*   **`src/routes/admin.ts` (or similar):** A new route handler for `POST /api/admin/profiles/:sourceId/clone` needs to be created here. This will parse the request body and call the core logic.
*   **`src/lib/profiles.service.ts` (or similar):** This new or existing service file should contain the main `cloneProfile` function. It will handle fetching source data, transforming it, and inserting it for the new profile.
*   **`shared/schema.ts`:** May need updates if new data access patterns or specific types are required for the cloning operation.

**2. Frappe DocTypes / Involved System Components:**
*   **API Endpoint:** `POST /api/admin/profiles/:sourceId/clone`
*   **Database Tables:** The operation centers on two tables:
    *   `rainbow_configs`: All configuration key-value pairs for a profile.
    *   `rainbow_kb_files`: Metadata for knowledge base files.

**3. Recommended Implementation Approach:**
1.  Implement the new `POST` endpoint in the admin API router.
2.  Create a service function that takes `sourceId`, `newProfileId`, and `displayName`.
3.  Inside the function, wrap the logic in a database transaction.
4.  Query all `rainbow_configs` and `rainbow_kb_files` rows for the `sourceId`.
5.  Filter out excluded keys (e.g., WhatsApp auth details, phone-specific settings).
6.  Map the results to new objects, replacing the `sourceId` with the `newProfileId`.
7.  Use a bulk-insert operation to write the new rows to both tables.
8.  Return `{ newProfileId }` on success.

**4. Edge Cases & Gotchas:**
*   **Atomicity:** The entire copy operation must be transactional to prevent partial clones.
*   **ID Uniqueness:** The endpoint must validate that the requested `newProfileId` does not already exist before proceeding.
*   **Data Exclusion:** Be explicit and careful when filtering out configs that must not be cloned, especially secrets and session data.
*   **No File I/O:** The operation is purely database-driven. Do not attempt to copy any files on disk (like KBs or WhatsApp sessions).



## Iteration 13 - Story: US-821 Complete webhook event routing to registered notification handlers
### What was implemented
- Created `src/routes/webhooks/handlers.ts` with:
  - `WebhookEvent` / `WebhookHandler` types
  - `handlerRegistry` — map of event type strings to async handler functions
  - `UnrecognizedEventError` class for clean error discrimination
  - `dispatchWebhookEvent()` — dispatches or throws UnrecognizedEventError
  - Handlers: `payment_confirmed` (sends WhatsApp via operator escalation), `booking_created`, `checkin_completed`, `checkout_completed`
- Updated `src/routes/webhooks/index.ts`:
  - Removed TODO from DIGIMAN endpoint — now dispatches to handler registry async (fire-and-forget after 200 ack)
  - Added `POST /webhooks/events` endpoint — synchronous dispatch, returns 422 for unrecognized types, 500 for handler errors
  - HMAC signature guard already in place via `signatureGuard` middleware
- Created `src/routes/webhooks/__tests__/webhook-routing.test.ts` — 7 tests:
  - dispatchWebhookEvent: correct dispatch, UnrecognizedEventError, re-throws handler errors
  - HTTP endpoint: 200 on success, 422 on unrecognized type, 500 on handler error, 400 on missing type field
### Patterns discovered
- `sendToOperatorWithEscalation(messageId, message, context?)` — takes messageId not operators array
- DIGIMAN-style endpoints ack 200 immediately (prevent retries) then dispatch async; handler errors are logged not re-raised
- The generic `/webhooks/events` endpoint is synchronous — allows callers to get meaningful HTTP status
- Mock `webhook-signature.js` in tests to bypass HMAC; test server uses `app.listen(0)` for random port
### Gotchas
- prd.json must be opened with `encoding='utf-8'` on Windows (cp1252 default fails on special chars)
- vitest `vi.resetModules()` in afterEach needed to avoid module registry pollution between HTTP tests
- Handler registry mutations in tests must restore original handlers in `finally` blocks
